# Montenegro personal income tax: figure ledger

Status: writer preparation. Independent review has not started. No accountant attestation.

The predecessor is frozen in `montenegro-income-tax-evidence/predecessor/`. All ten Guides and README have hashes; the nine unaffected Guides must remain byte-identical. The changed Guide has exactly two examples, and its nine other original examples are retained verbatim in a linked appendix.

Every numbered row below includes the full figure/rule text, section, act/article or form page, official URL, exact original-language excerpt and read date. The companion `.writer.json` retains source selectors and full-context hashes. Multiple rules in a paragraph are a group, not a claim that only one figure was checked.

## ME-PIT-001: 

This skill computes Montenegro personal income tax for an individual or a sole proprietor (preduzetnik) for 2026. The law is the Personal Income Tax Law (Zakon o porezu na dohodak fizičkih lica, "PIT Law"), consolidated through amendment 160/25, which applies from 1 January 2026 (160/25 art. 21), read with amendment 70/26, which changes the gambling rate from 21 May 2026 (70/26 arts. 1 and 3). The tax is run by the Tax Administration (Poreska uprava). The currency is the euro.

- Article 21; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 21 Na teret rashoda ne priznaju se: 1) troškovi koji nijesu nastali u svrhu obavljanja poslovne djelatnosti; 2) troškovi koji se ne mogu dokumentovati; 3) kamate za neblagovremeno plaćanje poreza i doprinosa; 4) kamate isplaćene nerezidentima, ako su plaćene po stopi višoj od uobičajene komercijalne stope; 5) troškovi na ime plaćanja izvršenih

## ME-PIT-002: Who this applies to

- **Taxpayers.** Every resident or nonresident individual who earns income from a source named in the law. Where two or more people earn income jointly, each is taxed on his or her share (PIT Law art. 2).

- Article 2; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 2 (1) Poreski obveznik poreza na dohodak je rezidentno, odnosno nerezidentno fizičko lice koje ostvari dohodak iz izvora utvrđenih ovim zakonom. (2) Kada dva ili više fizičkih lica zajednički ostvaruju dohodak, poreski obveznik je svako od tih lica, srazmjerno udjelu u dohotku koji je ostvarilo.

## ME-PIT-003: Who this applies to

- **Residents.** An individual is resident if he or she has a domicile or centre of business and vital interests in Montenegro, or stays in Montenegro for more than 183 days in the tax year. An individual sent abroad to work for a Montenegrin resident person or for an international organisation is also resident (art. 3). Citizenship, a bank account or one immigration document does not decide residence.

- Article 3; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 3 (1) Rezidentno fizičko lice (u daljem tekstu: rezident), u smislu ovog zakona, je fizičko lice koje: 1) na teritoriji Crne Gore ima prebivalište ili centar poslovnih i životnih interesa; 2) boravi na teritoriji Crne Gore više od 183 dana u poreskoj godini. (2) Rezident Crne Gore je i fizičko lice koje je upućeno izvan Crne Gore radi obavljanja posla

## ME-PIT-004: Who this applies to

- **Scope.** Residents are taxed on income earned in and outside Montenegro; nonresidents only on income earned in Montenegro (art. 4).

- Article 4; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 4 (1) Predmet oporezivanja rezidenta je dohodak koji rezident ostvari u Crnoj Gori i izvan Crne Gore. (2) Predmet oporezivanja nerezidentnog fizičkog lica (u daljem tekstu: nerezident) je dohodak koji nerezident ostvari u Crnoj Gori. 4. Prihodi koji se izuzimaju iz

## ME-PIT-005: Who this applies to

- **Tax period.** The calendar year, except where an activity starts or stops during the year (art. 7). On cessation of an independent activity, tax is computed for the part of the year in which it was carried on (art. 42(1a)).

- Article 7; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 7 (1) Poreski period za koji se obračunava porez na dohodak je kalendarska godina, osim u slučaju prestanka ili otpočinjanja djelatanosti u toku godine. (2) Porez na dohodak se obračunava nakon isteka kalendarske godine ili drugog perioda utvrđivanja poreza prema poreskoj osnovici ostvarenoj u tom periodu. 7. Poreska

## ME-PIT-006: Who this applies to

- **Income classes.** Personal earnings (salary); independent activity, split into a principal independent activity, supplementary entrepreneurship and occasional independent work; property (rent); capital; capital gains; sport; copyright, related rights, patents, trademarks and independent cultural professionals; other receipts; internet and video games (gaming); and games of chance (art. 12(2)). Total income is the sum of these for the period (art. 12(3)).

- Article 12; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 12 (1) Porez na dohodak plaća se na prihode iz svih izvora, osim onih koji su izuzeti ovim zakonom. (2) Izvore prihoda iz stava 1 ovog člana čine prihodi ostvareni po osnovu: 1) ličnih primanja; 2) samostalne djelatnosti, i to od: - osnovne samostalne djelatnosti; - dopunske preduzetničke djelatnosti; - povremene samostalne djelatnosti; 3) imovine; 4)

## ME-PIT-007: Who this applies to

- **Out of the tax.** Members of foreign diplomatic and consular missions and their households who are not Montenegrin citizens, UN technical-assistance officials, honorary consuls on pay from the appointing state, and officials, experts and administrative staff of international organisations who are not Montenegrin citizens or have no domicile in Montenegro (art. 6).

- Article 6; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 6 Porez na dohodak ne plaćaju sledeća lica: 1) članovi stranih diplomatskih misija u Crnoj Gori i članovi njihovih domaćinstava, ako nijesu državljani Crne Gore; 2) članovi konzularnih predstavništava kao i članovi njihovih domaćinstava, ako nijesu državljani Crne Gore; 3) funkcioneri i stručnjaci programa za tehničku pomoć Organizacije Ujedinjenih

## ME-PIT-040: Rates, bands and thresholds

Checks: a monthly salary of EUR 1,200 bears PIT of 300 × 9% + 200 × 15% = EUR 57.00. Annual principal-business profit of EUR 8,400 bears no tax; EUR 12,000 bears EUR 324.00. The salary bands are monthly; the business bands are annual. Never apply one to the other.

- Article 10; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 10 Stopa poreza na dohodak: 1) koji je ostvaren na osnovu prihoda iz člana 12 stav 2 tačka 1 ovog zakona iznosi: - 0% na iznos oporezivog dohotka do 700,00€, - 9% na iznos oporezivog dohotka od 700,01€ do 1.000,00€, - 15% na iznos oporezivog dohotka od 1.000,01€; 2) koji je ostavren na osnovu prihoda iz člana 12 stav 2 tačka 2 alineja 1 ovog zakona

## ME-PIT-041: How the base is built

Taxable income is total income less recognised expenses, which must be documented unless the law says otherwise (art. 13). A resident's base is the taxable income of the period less a carried-forward loss; a nonresident's base has no loss deduction (art. 8). Each income class has its own base, rate and payment route, so keep a separate schedule per class.

- An unidentified bank credit is neither an exempt gift nor automatically business revenue. If the tax authority finds undeclared income from the rise in net assets, art. 10a taxes it at 80%.

## ME-PIT-046: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Who.** A principal independent activity is a registered business, a liberal profession (health workers, vets, farmers, lawyers, notaries, auditors, engineers, architects, advisers, consultants, translators, journalists and similar) or professional and intellectual services that are the taxpayer's main activity (art. 16(2) and (3)). Supplementary entrepreneurship is business carried on by an entrepreneur as a supplementary activity under the company law (art. 16(4), added by [160/25 art. 5][amend]). Supplementary entrepreneurship is taxed at a flat 15%: it does not get the 0% and 9% bands.

## ME-PIT-047: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Base.** Taxable profit is the income-statement profit adjusted under the PIT Law (art. 17). Income and expenses follow the income statement under the accounting law unless the PIT Law says otherwise (arts. 18 and 20(1)). Dividends and profit shares from other legal persons are left out of the business base (art. 19); this does not exempt an individual's private dividends, which are capital income under art. 37.

## ME-PIT-048: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Not deductible** (art. 21): costs not incurred for the business, costs that cannot be documented, interest for late payment of tax and contributions, interest paid to nonresidents above the usual commercial rate, payments to a nonresident head office, profit distributions to employees or others, fines and penalties, and contributions to political organisations. Income tax and owner drawings are not expenses either.

## ME-PIT-049: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Inventory.** Materials and goods sold use weighted average cost or FIFO (art. 22).

## ME-PIT-050: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Depreciation.** Tax depreciation replaces book depreciation on fixed assets over EUR 200 with a life over one year, at the group rates in the table (art. 23). Right-of-use assets under operating leases and intangible assets are depreciated as booked under the accounting rules (art. 23(6) and (7)). Do not use the corporate-tax EUR 300 threshold.

## ME-PIT-051: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Capped expenses.** Qualifying-purpose spending up to 3% of total income, and only if paid to bodies carrying on those activities and used only for those purposes (art. 24). Representation up to 1%, if business-related, documented and not paid to a related person (art. 25). Memberships up to 0.1%, except a membership fixed by law (art. 26). Do not use the corporate 3.5% cap.

## ME-PIT-052: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Bad debts.** Deductible only if the debt was earlier included in income, is written off in the books and failed collection is proved; a later recovery goes back into income (art. 27). Reserve funds are not deductible (art. 28).

## ME-PIT-053: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Related-party interest.** Deductible up to the open-market interest cost and no more than actually paid (art. 30). The difference between arm's length interest and interest actually received is added to the lender's base (art. 31).

## ME-PIT-054: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Losses.** Business losses carry forward for no more than five years (art. 33). Keep a register by year of origin and use. A business loss is not set against rent, capital income or capital gains, and a capital loss is not carried forward under art. 33.

- Article 33; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 33 Gubici nastali iz poslovnih transakcija, mogu se prenijeti na račun dobiti iz budućeg perioda, ali ne duže od pet godina. 3. Prihodi od imovine

## ME-PIT-055: Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship

- **Mixed-use costs.** Home office, car, phone and internet costs are deductible only for the business part, since private costs are excluded (art. 21(1)) and costs must be documented (arts. 13(2) and 21(2)). The law sets no fixed home-office or car percentage: ask for floor-area or mileage evidence and deduct nothing until it is produced.

## ME-PIT-058: Occasional independent work

Occasional work outside the taxpayer's main activity, or a work contract (ugovor o djelu) to make or repair something or perform a physical or intellectual task, if not taxed under another heading (art. 16(5)). If costs are not documented, standard expenses are 30% of receipts (art. 20(2)), so the base is 70% of the gross receipt and tax is 15% of that. A legal-person or entrepreneur payer withholds the tax at each payment; income received directly is taxed with the annual return (art. 48a).

## ME-PIT-070: Property income

Rent from movable and immovable property (art. 34). Expenses are documented actual costs or, if not documented, 30% of rent (art. 35(1) and (2)). For rooms, apartments, houses and flats let to tourists, standard expenses are 50% if tourist tax was paid, or 70% under a lease with a travel agency or local tourist organisation giving average occupancy of at least 60 days a year (art. 35(3)). The route depends on the evidence, not on the larger percentage. Taxable income is rent less expenses in the same period (art. 36). A legal-person or entrepreneur tenant withholds 15% at each payment; rent received directly is taxed with the annual return (art. 49a). A licensed holiday-letting provider files a return even with no income in the year (art. 43(5)). Tourist tax, property tax and VAT are separate.

## ME-PIT-071: Capital income

Interest; the owner's private use of company assets and services; liquidation surplus above invested capital; dividends and profit shares in cash, shares or stakes; and shares that board members and employees receive or buy on preferential terms (art. 37(1) and (2), as amended by [160/25 art. 8][amend], which deleted point 2). No costs are deductible; the base is the gross amount (art. 37(3) and (4)). Interest on debt securities issued by the state, the Central Bank of Montenegro or a municipality is not capital income (art. 37(5)). The payer withholds 15% at payment (art. 50).

## ME-PIT-072: Capital gains

- **What.** Gains on the sale or transfer of real estate (land, buildings, flats, business units and other structures), stakes in legal persons and shares and other equity securities. A negative difference is a capital loss (art. 37a).

- Article 37a; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 37a (1) Kapitalnim dobitkom smatra se prihod ostvaren prodajom, odnosno prenosom imovine i to nepokretnosti, udjela u pravnom licu i hartija od vrijednosti (u daljem tekstu: imovina). (2) Oporezivi prihod po osnovu kapitalnog dobitka predstavlja razliku između prodajne i nabavne cijene imovine iz stava 1 ovog člana. (3) Negativna razlika iz stava 2

## ME-PIT-073: Capital gains

- **Real estate.** Sale price is the contract price less documented reconstruction and sale costs; the tax authority sets the price if the contract price is below market, and an exchange uses market price (art. 37c). Acquisition cost is the price paid or the price the authority set on acquisition under the real-estate transfer tax law, or construction cost for a self-built property; for a gift or inheritance, the donor's or deceased's cost. It is indexed each year by retail price growth from acquisition to sale, using official statistics. Only if no cost can be established is acquisition cost 80% of the sale price (art. 37d).

## ME-PIT-075: Capital gains

- **Shares.** Listed shares cost the documented price paid; unlisted shares the documented price, or nominal value if no price is documented. Repeated purchases of the same issuer use FIFO. Bonus shares paid as a dividend cost the value at which they were acquired (art. 37f).

## ME-PIT-079: Sport, copyright, other receipts, internet and gaming

- **Sport** (arts. 37i to 37k): income of professional and amateur athletes, coaches and sports professionals, transfers and fees, that is not salary. Documented costs, or standard 30%; amateur athletes 90%.

## ME-PIT-082: Sport, copyright, other receipts, internet and gaming

- **Internet and video games** (arts. 37o to 37r): income from an activity carried on through the internet, if not taxed under another heading, with a fixed 15% expense; income from video-game competitions and other gaming that is not salary, with documented costs or 20% standard. These percentages are expenses; the tax rate is 15%. Do not use both actual and standard costs, and do not move salary or business income into the internet heading.

## ME-PIT-083: Gambling winnings

Taxable winnings are each individual winning from lottery games, betting and global jackpots, less the stake for that winning (art. 37s, as replaced by [160/25 art. 10][amend]). Prize games are not income (art. 5(10)). Rates: progressive bands for winnings paid to 20 May 2026; 12% for winnings paid from 21 May 2026 ([70/26][may]). The game organiser withholds at each payment or transfer (art. 50f).

## ME-PIT-084: Reliefs

- **Underdeveloped municipalities** (arts. 32 and 32a): a taxpayer who starts an activity there pays no tax for the first eight years from registration, capped at EUR 200,000 in total including other state aid. Not available to someone who closed the same or a similar activity in the previous three years, to recipients of aid for firms in difficulty, or in transport, shipbuilding, steel, trade and hospitality (except primary hospitality establishments). Apply within 30 days of registration; the authority decides; a statement of other state aid goes with each annual return.

## ME-PIT-085: Reliefs

- **New hires there** (art. 32b): a permanent or at least five-year hire of a person registered with the Employment Agency for over three months and living in an underdeveloped municipality frees the salary tax for four years, if headcount rises above the 12-month average. If the employer ends the contract within five years (other than at the employee's request or for serious breach), the tax is repaid within 30 days.

## ME-PIT-088: What sits on top of PIT

- **Municipal surtax (prirez).** A municipality can levy a surtax on the PIT on salary, independent activity, property and property rights, and capital income, at up to 13% of the tax, or up to 15% in the Capital City and the Old Royal Capital (Local Self-Government Financing Law art. 8). The actual rate is the municipality's own decision (art. 9). It is a tax on the tax, never added to the income rate: at a 13% surtax the top business rate becomes 15% × 1.13 = 16.95%; at 15% it is 17.25%. The law lists only these four bases; capital gains and gambling winnings are not among them.

## ME-PIT-089: What sits on top of PIT

- **Social contributions.** Employees pay 10% PIO and 0.5% unemployment; the employer pays 0% PIO and 0.5% unemployment (Contributions Law arts. 15 and 18). The health contribution provisions are deleted (art. 17). The annual PIO ceiling is set each year by Ministry of Finance act (art. 14(3) and (4)). Older secondary guides still show 15% employee pension and 8.5% health; those rates are out of date. Details, bases and the self-employed rules are in the [social contributions skill](montenegro-social-contributions.md).

## ME-PIT-090: What sits on top of PIT

- **VAT.** Standard rate 21% (VAT Law art. 24); a business whose turnover in the last 12 months, or a shorter period, exceeds EUR 30,000 must apply for registration by the 20th of the following month (arts. 42 and 55). A VAT-registered taxpayer cannot pay lump-sum PIT (PIT Law art. 49(12)). See the [VAT skill](montenegro-vat.md).

## ME-PIT-091: Deadlines and filing

- **Annual return.** Form GPP-FL with schedules A, PPG, A1, B, B1, C, D, D1, E, E1, F, G and H ([return rulebook art. 3][return]), filed by the end of April for the previous year (PIT Law art. 43(2)). Tax is self-assessed and paid when the return is filed; advances and withholding on all sources are credited (art. 42(2), (4) and (5)). An overpayment is refunded on request or credited to the next year's advances (art. 42(6)).

## ME-PIT-092: Deadlines and filing

- **Who files** (art. 43(4) to (6)): residents with a principal independent activity (unless on lump-sum tax), supplementary entrepreneurship, or occasional work not taxed by withholding; rent, unless the payer is a legal person or entrepreneur; capital gains; foreign income; salary from two or more payers with total monthly gross above EUR 700; sport; copyright and related income; internet and gaming income. A licensed holiday-letting provider files even with no income. A nonresident files for Montenegrin income on which no withholding is prescribed.

## ME-PIT-096: Deadlines and filing

- **Two or more employers.** The authority assesses the tax by decision within 30 days of the return, from the return and its own records (art. 43a).

## ME-PIT-097: Deadlines and filing

- **Business advances.** Principal and supplementary business tax is paid in monthly advances of 1/12 of the previous year's tax, by the end of each month for the previous month (art. 48(1) and (2)). A new business files an information return with its estimated income within 15 days of registration (art. 48(4)).

## ME-PIT-098: Deadlines and filing

- **Lump-sum tax** (art. 49): available on request to a principal independent activity with turnover below EUR 30,000.01. Apply by the end of January, or within five days of registration for a new activity; the authority decides within 15 days. Tax is paid monthly at 1/12 by the end of the month for the previous month; seasonal activities of up to six months pay by the 5th of the month for the current month. Turnover books must be kept. Not available for lawyers, notaries, auditors, accountants, health, consulting, design, surveying, enforcement officers and other professional and intellectual activities, hairdressing, billiards and amusement games, wholesale and retail trade, hospitality, hotels, financial intermediation, real estate, or VAT-registered taxpayers; the authority can approve lump-sum tax for trade or hospitality in a kiosk, trailer or mobile unit only where the taxpayer produces and sells only its own products (art. 49(10) and (11)). If circumstances justify it, the authority orders a change of method by decision (art. 49(7)). The scale is set by Ministry rulebook (art. 49(2)).

## ME-PIT-102: Deadlines and filing

- **Employer reports.** Monthly report of taxes and contributions by the 15th of the month for the previous month (art. 47(3)); annual statement of salary and tax to each employee by 31 January (art. 47(1)); other payers give recipients a statement by 31 January (art. 47(5)).

(2) Poslodavac je dužan da nadležnom poreskom organu dostavlja izvještaje i druge evidencije koje su bitne za obračun poreza na lična

## ME-PIT-104: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

First apply the principal-activity, profession, VAT and own-product gates in article 49 above. A turnover of exactly EUR 30,000 is within the statutory test of less than EUR 30,000.01; a turnover of EUR 30,000.01 is not. A group name in the rulebook does not override a statutory exclusion. For example, the rulebook's group-I list includes barbers, while the statute excludes hairdressing. Do not infer eligibility from the group list alone. The authority fixes the liability by decision. [PIT Law art. 49][law]; [Pausal Rulebook arts. 2 to 5][pausal].

## ME-PIT-105: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

The four activity groups recognise costs of I: 50%, II: 40%, III: 35%, IV: 30%. The rulebook's examples include beekeepers in I, tailors and plumbers in II, vehicle mechanics in III, and bakers in IV. Match the actual activity to the full article 3 list and obtain the decision; do not choose the largest cost percentage. Within each group the turnover ceilings are EUR 7,500, 15,000, 22,500 and 30,000. The following are the **annual scale amounts**, using the subgroup ceiling, not a calculation of costs against arbitrary actual receipts. [Pausal Rulebook arts. 3 to 5][pausal].

## ME-PIT-122: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

Column 5 of the official scale is PIT alone. Column 6 is PIT **plus mandatory social contributions**, shown as `taxable income × 11% + PIT`. For an ordinary insured principal entrepreneur this reconciles to 10% PIO plus 1% unemployment on the pausal taxable-income base (Contributions Law arts. 9(7), 11(5), 15(2) and 18(2)). Do not treat the combined column as PIT or add those contributions a second time. Municipal surtax is separate and uses the relevant municipal decision. [Pausal Rulebook art. 5][pausal]; [Contributions Law][contrib]; [2026 seasonal informator, pausal section][seasonal].

## ME-PIT-123: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

For a full-year group-II third-subgroup decision, annual PIT is EUR 549 and contributions EUR 1,485, total EUR 2,034; the corresponding monthly amounts are EUR 45.75, 123.75 and 169.50. The ordinary PIT payment is one twelfth by the end of the current month for the previous month; the special payment date for a seasonal principal activity of no more than six months is the fifth of the current month. Use the authority's actual period and decision for a seasonal or partial-year liability, rather than assuming the full annual table or a rounding method. [PIT Law art. 49(3), (6) and (8)][law]; [Pausal Rulebook art. 5][pausal].

## ME-PIT-124: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

**ZPO application.** The printed application records taxpayer identity and addresses, business details, bank accounts, year, activity and manner of working, owned/rented premises and area, owned/rented equipment, employee count, prior-year product and service receipts and their sum (7.1 + 7.2 = 7.3), planned product and service receipts and their sum (8.1 + 8.2 = 8.3), applicant details and signature/date, and lodging details. Section 11 is completed by the authority. The ordinary deadlines remain end January or five days after a new registration. For **2026 only**, the Tax Administration treated applications through **27 February 2026** as timely and required paper ZPO during the IRMS rollout. Its later 2026 seasonal informator describes electronic ZPOG/ZPOS applications in IRMS's collection module. Preserve this chronology; the printed form's diskette/email fields are not evidence that those channels operate today. [Pausal Rulebook art. 7 and ZPO attachment][pausal]; [Tax Administration notice, 5 February 2026][pausalnotice]; [2026 seasonal informator][seasonal].

## ME-PIT-125: Lump-sum scale, application and turnover book

**KPP turnover book.** Record name, place, activity/group and tax ID; then each transaction's serial number, chronological posting date, product receipts, service receipts and their total (columns 3 + 4 = 5). Article 6 requires the record to use turnover registered through the electronic collection device and allows the book itself to be electronic. Reconcile the product/service totals to the application and decision records. [Pausal Rulebook art. 6 and KPP attachment][pausal].

## ME-PIT-128: GPP-FL: promulgated schedules and the calculation crosswalk

| A: independent-activity profit/loss | Start with accounting profit (1), add book depreciation (2) and nonallowable expenses (4), deduct tax depreciation (3) and dividends/profit shares included in the business accounts (5). Positive adjusted profit is row 6; deduct permitted PPG losses (7) to obtain row 8. Row 9 separates the taxable bands and feeds main 1.1; the annual principal-activity tax goes to main 1.2. A new business loss belongs to A10, not a PIT credit. | [103/25 art. 3, A and instructions][gppforms]; [PIT Law arts. 17 to 33][law] |

## ME-PIT-141: GPP-FL: promulgated schedules and the calculation crosswalk

**Main settlement.** The printed form separately calculates principal-business PIT (1.2) and foreign-salary PIT (2.2). Foreign salary uses the monthly salary bands on each month's receipts, not the annual business scale. The bases in main 3.1 to 3.6 sum to 3.7; the printed 15% row 3.8 produces 3.9. Main 4.1 sums 1.2 + 2.2 + 3.9. Main 5.1 to 5.7 record paid business PIT advances, approved underdeveloped-municipality and research/innovation relief, B/D/E withholding and the allowed F credit; total at 5.8. Do not enter a contribution payment as a PIT advance. A positive 4.1 minus 5.8 goes to 6.1 (due); the reverse positive difference to 6.2 (overpaid). Split an overpayment between refund requested (6.3) and next-period credit (6.4) without claiming it twice. [GPP-FL main calculation and instructions, F instructions][gppforms]; [PIT Law arts. 42 and 44][law].

## ME-PIT-142: GPP-FL: promulgated schedules and the calculation crosswalk

**Foreign-tax evidence.** F's instructions require a certificate from the competent authority abroad showing the source, amount of income and tax paid, in the source-country language and Montenegrin with a court-certified translation. Preserve the proof and apply treaty priority and the article 44 cap; the printed aggregate credit field does not authorise unused credit from one income source to erase tax on another. [F instructions][gppforms]; [PIT Law arts. 44 and 45][law].

## ME-PIT-143: GPP-FL: promulgated schedules and the calculation crosswalk

**Printed-instruction conflicts.** The A instructions tell the reader once to put a loss in row 9, whereas the actual form and its later instruction use row 10. A1's instruction for total contributions says `2.1 + 2.2`, even though 2.2 is a PIO exemption and unemployment has separate rows. F's prose for row 11 points to row 2 column 6 (tax), while the printed form points to row 2 column 5 (income). F's discussion of the domestic-tax cap also refers to row 13 where the actual cap is row 14. A general note says occasional work is not entered in GPP-FL, but the same promulgated amendment supplies H for nonwithheld occasional receipts; H's following-page prose also contradicts the statutory payer-withholding duty. Preserve these discrepancies in the workpaper, follow the current statute for liability, and do not present an invented portal correction as verified. [103/25 art. 3, main notes and A/A1/F/H instructions][gppforms]; [PIT Law arts. 43 and 48a][law]. The unresolved 2026 mappings and rounding are listed under Sources.

## ME-PIT-150: Cross-border points

- **Foreign tax credit.** A resident who pays income tax abroad gets a credit for it, capped at the Montenegrin tax on that income (art. 44). Compute the cap with the right class rate and base: a principal business uses the progressive bands, not a flat 15%. Do not credit social contributions, refundable treaty excess or unproved tax. The law gives no carry-forward of unused credit.

- Article 44; https://wapi.gov.me/download/b8465a42-2073-4894-9be0-3b965c2f7195?version=1.0; read 2026-10-31; original quote: Član 44 (1) Rezidentnom poreskom obvezniku koji ostvari dohodak izvan Crne Gore i koji plaća porez na dohodak drugoj državi odobrava se poreski kredit u visini poreza na dohodak plaćenog u toj državi. (2) Poreski kredit iz stava 1 ovog člana ne može biti veći od iznosa koji bi se dobio primjenom odredaba ovog zakona na dohodak ostvaren u drugoj

## ME-PIT-157: 1. Principal business: accounts to tax, and the annual balance

Total revenue 80,000; accounting profit 25,000 after all booked costs. Add back private costs 1,200 and book depreciation above tax depreciation 800. Qualifying-purpose spending 3,000 exceeds the 3% cap (2,400) by 600; representation 1,000 exceeds 1% (800) by 200; memberships 120 exceed 0.1% (80) by 40. Adjusted profit is 27,840. An unused business loss of 3,000 within its five years brings the base to 24,840. PIT is 3,600 × 9% = 324 plus 12,840 × 15% = 1,926, total 2,250. Advances paid 1,800, so 450 is due with the return. [Arts. 17 to 33, 42 and 48][law]

## ME-PIT-166: Retained example 10. Sole proprietor with municipal surtax

Principal-business taxable profit 20,000; the municipality's surtax decision sets 13%. PIT is 324 + 8,000 × 15% = 1,524. Surtax is 13% of the PIT, 198.12. Total 1,722.12. Contributions are computed separately. [PIT Law art. 10(1)(2)][law]; [Local Self-Government Financing Law arts. 8 and 9][local]

## ME-PIT-167: Retained example 11. Gambling winning before and after 21 May 2026

A betting winning of 1,500 after deducting the stake. Paid on 10 March 2026, the bands apply: 1,450 × 10% = 145. Paid on 10 June 2026, the flat rate applies: 1,500 × 12% = 180. | 3 | description: "Montenegro personal income tax (porez na dohodak fizičkih lica) for tax year 2026, from the Personal Income Tax Law as amended by 160/25 (from 1 January 2026) and 70/26 (from 21 May 2026). Covers residence and scope, the monthly salary bands (0, 9 and 15% at EUR 700 and 1,000), the annual bands for a principal independent activity (0, 9 and 15% at EUR 8,400 and 12,000), the flat 15% for supplementary entrepreneurs, occasional work, rent, capital income, capital gains, sport, royalties, other receipts and internet or gaming income, and gambling winnings (progressive to 20 May, 12% from 21 May). Sets out business-profit adjustments, standard expense percentages, losses, reliefs, the municipal surtax, the GPP-FL return due by the end of April, monthly advances, withholding, lump-sum tax, fines and the foreign tax credit. Payroll, social contributions and VAT have their own skills." | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 17 | # Montenegro personal income tax, tax year 2026 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 19 | This skill computes Montenegro personal income tax for an individual or a sole proprietor (preduzetnik) for 2026. The law is the Personal Income Tax Law (Zakon o porezu na dohodak fizičkih lica, "PIT Law"), consolidated through amendment 160/25, which applies from 1 January 2026 (160/25 art. 21), read with amendment 70/26, which changes the gambling rate from 21 May 2026 (70/26 arts. 1 and 3). The tax is run by the Tax Administration (Poreska uprava). The currency is the euro. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 21 | ## Who this applies to | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 23 | - **Taxpayers.** Every resident or nonresident individual who earns income from a source named in the law. Where two or more people earn income jointly, each is taxed on his or her share (PIT Law art. 2). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 24 | - **Residents.** An individual is resident if he or she has a domicile or centre of business and vital interests in Montenegro, or stays in Montenegro for more than 183 days in the tax year. An individual sent abroad to work for a Montenegrin resident person or for an international organisation is also resident (art. 3). Citizenship, a bank account or one immigration document does not decide residence. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 25 | - **Scope.** Residents are taxed on income earned in and outside Montenegro; nonresidents only on income earned in Montenegro (art. 4). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 26 | - **Tax period.** The calendar year, except where an activity starts or stops during the year (art. 7). On cessation of an independent activity, tax is computed for the part of the year in which it was carried on (art. 42(1a)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 27 | - **Income classes.** Personal earnings (salary); independent activity, split into a principal independent activity, supplementary entrepreneurship and occasional independent work; property (rent); capital; capital gains; sport; copyright, related rights, patents, trademarks and independent cultural professionals; other receipts; internet and video games (gaming); and games of chance (art. 12(2)). Total income is the sum of these for the period (art. 12(3)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 28 | - **Out of the tax.** Members of foreign diplomatic and consular missions and their households who are not Montenegrin citizens, UN technical-assistance officials, honorary consuls on pay from the appointing state, and officials, experts and administrative staff of international organisations who are not Montenegrin citizens or have no domicile in Montenegro (art. 6). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 29 | - **Other skills.** Monthly salary withholding and the IOPPD report are in the [payroll skill](montenegro-payroll.md); insurance status, contribution bases and the annual pension refund in the [social contributions skill](montenegro-social-contributions.md); VAT in the [VAT skill](montenegro-vat.md); companies (d.o.o., a.d.) in the [corporate income tax skill](me-corporate-income-tax.md). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 31 | ## Rates, bands and thresholds | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 35 | \| Personal earnings (salary), monthly \| 0% up to EUR 700.00; 9% from EUR 700.01 to 1,000.00; 15% above EUR 1,000.00 \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 10(1)(1)][law], as restated by [160/25 art. 3][amend] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 36 | \| Principal independent activity, annual \| 0% up to EUR 8,400.00; 9% from EUR 8,400.01 to 12,000.00; 15% above EUR 12,000.00 \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 10(1)(2)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 37 | \| Supplementary entrepreneurship, occasional work, property, capital, capital gains, sport, royalties, other receipts, internet and gaming \| 15% of the tax base \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 10(1)(3)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 38 | \| Gambling winnings paid 1 January to 20 May 2026 \| 0% up to EUR 50.00; 10% from EUR 50.01 to 1,500.00; 15% above EUR 1,500.00, per winning \| 1 Jan to 20 May 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 10(1)(4)][law], [160/25 art. 3][amend] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 46 | \| Health, humanitarian, social, educational, scientific, religious, cultural, sports and environmental spending \| deductible up to 3% of total income \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 24][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 47 | \| Representation (entertainment) \| deductible up to 1% of total income \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 25][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 48 | \| Chamber and association memberships \| deductible up to 0.1% of total income, or the amount fixed by law \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 26][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 51 | \| New hire in an underdeveloped municipality \| salary tax relief for 4 years from hiring \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 32b][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 52 | \| Property acquisition cost that cannot be established \| 80% of the sale price \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 37d(5)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 53 | \| Sport income, undocumented costs \| standard expenses 30%; amateur athletes 90% \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 37j][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 55 | \| Internet income \| expenses 15% of receipts \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 37p(1)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 56 | \| Video-game (gaming) income, undocumented costs \| standard expenses 20% \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 37p(3)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 58 | \| Salary from two or more payers, return required \| total monthly gross above EUR 700 \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 43(4)(5)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 60 | \| Pension and disability contribution (PIO), employees \| 10% employee, 0% employer \| 2026 \| [Contributions Law art. 15][contrib] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 61 | \| Unemployment contribution, employees \| 0.5% employee, 0.5% employer \| 2026 \| [Contributions Law art. 18][contrib] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 62 | \| VAT, standard rate and registration \| 21%; registration once turnover in the last 12 months, or a shorter period, exceeds EUR 30,000 \| 2026 \| [VAT Law arts. 24, 42 and 55][vat] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 64 | \| Fine, entrepreneur (return, advances, information return, lump sum, books) \| EUR 1,000 to 6,000, plus a possible ban on the activity of 1 to 3 months \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 53a(3) and (4)][law]; [160/25 art. 18][amend] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 65 | \| Fine, legal-person payer \| EUR 2,000 to 20,000; responsible person EUR 500 to 1,000 \| 2026 \| [PIT Law art. 53a(1) and (2)][law] \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 67 | Checks: a monthly salary of EUR 1,200 bears PIT of 300 × 9% + 200 × 15% = EUR 57.00. Annual principal-business profit of EUR 8,400 bears no tax; EUR 12,000 bears EUR 324.00. The salary bands are monthly; the business bands are annual. Never apply one to the other. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 69 | ## The taxable base | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 71 | ### How the base is built | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 73 | Taxable income is total income less recognised expenses, which must be documented unless the law says otherwise (art. 13). A resident's base is the taxable income of the period less a carried-forward loss; a nonresident's base has no loss deduction (art. 8). Each income class has its own base, rate and payment route, so keep a separate schedule per class. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 75 | ### Exclusions and exempt amounts | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 77 | - **Not income** (art. 5): disability rights payments, child allowance, basic social-protection rights, aid for natural disasters, health-insurance benefits other than salary compensation, inheritances and gifts, organised humanitarian aid, state awards, pensions (except pensions of state and public officials), prize games, insurance of property and life, and receipts of religious officials (point 12, added by [160/25 art. 1][amend]). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 78 | - **Exempt up to set amounts** (art. 5a): for example, death solidarity aid up to EUR 2,000 (raised from 1,500 by [160/25 art. 2][amend]), illness aid up to EUR 1,000, retirement severance up to EUR 1,500, field allowance and separated-living allowance up to EUR 100 a month, student grants up to EUR 300 a month, newborn payment to an employee up to EUR 1,000, amateur athletes' food allowance up to EUR 600 a month, and jubilee awards of EUR 150 to 450. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 79 | - An inherited or gifted property's exclusion at receipt does not make its later sale tax free: the acquisition cost is then the donor's or deceased's cost (art. 37d(3)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 80 | - An unidentified bank credit is neither an exempt gift nor automatically business revenue. If the tax authority finds undeclared income from the rise in net assets, art. 10a taxes it at 80%. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 82 | ### Principal independent activity and supplementary entrepreneurship | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 84 | - **Who.** A principal independent activity is a registered business, a liberal profession (health workers, vets, farmers, lawyers, notaries, auditors, engineers, architects, advisers, consultants, translators, journalists and similar) or professional and intellectual services that are the taxpayer's main activity (art. 16(2) and (3)). Supplementary entrepreneurship is business carried on by an entrepreneur as a supplementary activity under the company law (art. 16(4), added by [160/25 art. 5][amend]). Supplementary entrepreneurship is taxed at a flat 15%: it does not get the 0% and 9% bands. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 85 | - **Base.** Taxable profit is the income-statement profit adjusted under the PIT Law (art. 17). Income and expenses follow the income statement under the accounting law unless the PIT Law says otherwise (arts. 18 and 20(1)). Dividends and profit shares from other legal persons are left out of the business base (art. 19); this does not exempt an individual's private dividends, which are capital income under art. 37. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 86 | - **Not deductible** (art. 21): costs not incurred for the business, costs that cannot be documented, interest for late payment of tax and contributions, interest paid to nonresidents above the usual commercial rate, payments to a nonresident head office, profit distributions to employees or others, fines and penalties, and contributions to political organisations. Income tax and owner drawings are not expenses either. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 87 | - **Inventory.** Materials and goods sold use weighted average cost or FIFO (art. 22). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 88 | - **Depreciation.** Tax depreciation replaces book depreciation on fixed assets over EUR 200 with a life over one year, at the group rates in the table (art. 23). Right-of-use assets under operating leases and intangible assets are depreciated as booked under the accounting rules (art. 23(6) and (7)). Do not use the corporate-tax EUR 300 threshold. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 89 | - **Capped expenses.** Qualifying-purpose spending up to 3% of total income, and only if paid to bodies carrying on those activities and used only for those purposes (art. 24). Representation up to 1%, if business-related, documented and not paid to a related person (art. 25). Memberships up to 0.1%, except a membership fixed by law (art. 26). Do not use the corporate 3.5% cap. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 90 | - **Bad debts.** Deductible only if the debt was earlier included in income, is written off in the books and failed collection is proved; a later recovery goes back into income (art. 27). Reserve funds are not deductible (art. 28). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 91 | - **Related-party interest.** Deductible up to the open-market interest cost and no more than actually paid (art. 30). The difference between arm's length interest and interest actually received is added to the lender's base (art. 31). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 92 | - **Losses.** Business losses carry forward for no more than five years (art. 33). Keep a register by year of origin and use. A business loss is not set against rent, capital income or capital gains, and a capital loss is not carried forward under art. 33. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 93 | - **Mixed-use costs.** Home office, car, phone and internet costs are deductible only for the business part, since private costs are excluded (art. 21(1)) and costs must be documented (arts. 13(2) and 21(2)). The law sets no fixed home-office or car percentage: ask for floor-area or mileage evidence and deduct nothing until it is produced. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 94 | - **VAT-registered taxpayers.** Output VAT is not income and recoverable input VAT is not an expense, because the base follows the income statement (arts. 18 and 20). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 96 | Principal annual business PIT on taxable base B: `0.09 × max(min(B, 12000) − 8400, 0) + 0.15 × max(B − 12000, 0)`. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 98 | ### Occasional independent work | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 100 | Occasional work outside the taxpayer's main activity, or a work contract (ugovor o djelu) to make or repair something or perform a physical or intellectual task, if not taxed under another heading (art. 16(5)). If costs are not documented, standard expenses are 30% of receipts (art. 20(2)), so the base is 70% of the gross receipt and tax is 15% of that. A legal-person or entrepreneur payer withholds the tax at each payment; income received directly is taxed with the annual return (art. 48a). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 102 | ### Classifying bank-statement lines | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 104 | \| Line on the statement \| Class \| Rule \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 105 | \|---\|---\|---\| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 106 | \| Client payments, fees, platform payouts (Stripe, PayPal, Wise, Upwork) into a registered business \| Principal or supplementary business income \| Arts. 16 to 18 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 107 | \| ZARADA, PLATA, salary from an employer \| Personal earnings, monthly bands \| Arts. 10(1)(1), 14 and 15 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 108 | \| ZAKUP, KIRIJA, rent received \| Property income, 15% after expenses \| Arts. 34 to 36 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 109 | \| KAMATA, interest received \| Capital income, 15% of gross (interest on Montenegrin government, central bank or municipal debt securities is not capital income) \| Art. 37(1)(1) and (5) \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 110 | \| DIVIDENDA, dividend \| Capital income, 15% of gross \| Art. 37(1)(4) \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 111 | \| POVRAĆAJ POREZA, tax refund \| Not income \| Art. 42(6) \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 112 | \| Own-account transfers, loan principal, VAT payments \| Neither income nor expense \| Arts. 13 and 18 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 113 | \| POREZ, PRIREZ, income tax paid; drawings \| Not deductible \| Art. 21 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 114 | \| KAZNA, fines and penalties \| Not deductible \| Art. 21(7) \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 115 | \| Laptop, furniture, car over EUR 200 \| Fixed asset, depreciate \| Art. 23 \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 116 | \| DOPRINOSI, PIO, contributions \| Track separately in the contribution workpaper \| Contributions Law \| | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 118 | ### Property income | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 120 | Rent from movable and immovable property (art. 34). Expenses are documented actual costs or, if not documented, 30% of rent (art. 35(1) and (2)). For rooms, apartments, houses and flats let to tourists, standard expenses are 50% if tourist tax was paid, or 70% under a lease with a travel agency or local tourist organisation giving average occupancy of at least 60 days a year (art. 35(3)). The route depends on the evidence, not on the larger percentage. Taxable income is rent less expenses in the same period (art. 36). A legal-person or entrepreneur tenant withholds 15% at each payment; rent received directly is taxed with the annual return (art. 49a). A licensed holiday-letting provider files a return even with no income in the year (art. 43(5)). Tourist tax, property tax and VAT are separate. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 122 | ### Capital income | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 124 | Interest; the owner's private use of company assets and services; liquidation surplus above invested capital; dividends and profit shares in cash, shares or stakes; and shares that board members and employees receive or buy on preferential terms (art. 37(1) and (2), as amended by [160/25 art. 8][amend], which deleted point 2). No costs are deductible; the base is the gross amount (art. 37(3) and (4)). Interest on debt securities issued by the state, the Central Bank of Montenegro or a municipality is not capital income (art. 37(5)). The payer withholds 15% at payment (art. 50). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 126 | ### Capital gains | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 128 | - **What.** Gains on the sale or transfer of real estate (land, buildings, flats, business units and other structures), stakes in legal persons and shares and other equity securities. A negative difference is a capital loss (art. 37a). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 129 | - **Real estate.** Sale price is the contract price less documented reconstruction and sale costs; the tax authority sets the price if the contract price is below market, and an exchange uses market price (art. 37c). Acquisition cost is the price paid or the price the authority set on acquisition under the real-estate transfer tax law, or construction cost for a self-built property; for a gift or inheritance, the donor's or deceased's cost. It is indexed each year by retail price growth from acquisition to sale, using official statistics. Only if no cost can be established is acquisition cost 80% of the sale price (art. 37d). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 130 | - **Company stakes.** Sale and purchase values are those in the contract; the authority sets the sale value if below market; a stake acquired on founding costs the founding contribution plus increases (art. 37e). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 131 | - **Shares.** Listed shares cost the documented price paid; unlisted shares the documented price, or nominal value if no price is documented. Repeated purchases of the same issuer use FIFO. Bonus shares paid as a dividend cost the value at which they were acquired (art. 37f). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 132 | - **Exempt** (art. 37g): a property that was the taxpayer's only and main residence (domicile); transfers between spouses or life partners linked to marriage, partnership, divorce, its end or inheritance; gifts to first-order heirs. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 133 | - **Losses.** A capital loss is set only against capital gains of the same tax period (art. 37h). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 134 | - **Tax.** 15%, computed annually and paid with the annual return (art. 50a). A gain recorded in business books goes in the tax balance, not a separate return (art. 43(6a)); never report it twice. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 136 | ### Sport, copyright, other receipts, internet and gaming | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 138 | - **Sport** (arts. 37i to 37k): income of professional and amateur athletes, coaches and sports professionals, transfers and fees, that is not salary. Documented costs, or standard 30%; amateur athletes 90%. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 139 | - **Copyright, related rights, patents, trademarks, cultural professionals** (arts. 37l to 37m): documented costs, or standard 60% for fine art, 50% for architecture, urban and landscape planning, 30% for performers' rights and 30% for everything else. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 140 | - **Other receipts** (art. 37n): awards and payments to people not employed by the payer, officials' pensions under the officials' salary law (point 1a, added by [160/25 art. 9][amend]), receipts above art. 5a limits, pay of board and assembly members not employed by the payer, bankruptcy trustees and lay judges, severance on agreed termination, sales-agency income and all other untaxed income. Taxed on gross (art. 37n(3)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 141 | - **Internet and video games** (arts. 37o to 37r): income from an activity carried on through the internet, if not taxed under another heading, with a fixed 15% expense; income from video-game competitions and other gaming that is not salary, with documented costs or 20% standard. These percentages are expenses; the tax rate is 15%. Do not use both actual and standard costs, and do not move salary or business income into the internet heading. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 143 | ### Gambling winnings | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 145 | Taxable winnings are each individual winning from lottery games, betting and global jackpots, less the stake for that winning (art. 37s, as replaced by [160/25 art. 10][amend]). Prize games are not income (art. 5(10)). Rates: progressive bands for winnings paid to 20 May 2026; 12% for winnings paid from 21 May 2026 ([70/26][may]). The game organiser withholds at each payment or transfer (art. 50f). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 147 | ### Reliefs | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 149 | - **Underdeveloped municipalities** (arts. 32 and 32a): a taxpayer who starts an activity there pays no tax for the first eight years from registration, capped at EUR 200,000 in total including other state aid. Not available to someone who closed the same or a similar activity in the previous three years, to recipients of aid for firms in difficulty, or in transport, shipbuilding, steel, trade and hospitality (except primary hospitality establishments). Apply within 30 days of registration; the authority decides; a statement of other state aid goes with each annual return. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 150 | - **New hires there** (art. 32b): a permanent or at least five-year hire of a person registered with the Employment Agency for over three months and living in an underdeveloped municipality frees the salary tax for four years, if headcount rises above the 12-month average. If the employer ends the contract within five years (other than at the employee's request or for serious breach), the tax is repaid within 30 days. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 151 | - **Expired** (art. 32c): the 2021 to 2023 new-hire relief. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 152 | - **Research and innovation; digital nomads** (art. 32d): exemption for beneficiaries of research and innovation incentives, and for income from a foreign employer earned under digital-nomad status, each under its own law. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 154 | ### What sits on top of PIT | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 156 | - **Municipal surtax (prirez).** A municipality can levy a surtax on the PIT on salary, independent activity, property and property rights, and capital income, at up to 13% of the tax, or up to 15% in the Capital City and the Old Royal Capital (Local Self-Government Financing Law art. 8). The actual rate is the municipality's own decision (art. 9). It is a tax on the tax, never added to the income rate: at a 13% surtax the top business rate becomes 15% × 1.13 = 16.95%; at 15% it is 17.25%. The law lists only these four bases; capital gains and gambling winnings are not among them. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 157 | - **Social contributions.** Employees pay 10% PIO and 0.5% unemployment; the employer pays 0% PIO and 0.5% unemployment (Contributions Law arts. 15 and 18). The health contribution provisions are deleted (art. 17). The annual PIO ceiling is set each year by Ministry of Finance act (art. 14(3) and (4)). Older secondary guides still show 15% employee pension and 8.5% health; those rates are out of date. Details, bases and the self-employed rules are in the [social contributions skill](montenegro-social-contributions.md). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 158 | - **VAT.** Standard rate 21% (VAT Law art. 24); a business whose turnover in the last 12 months, or a shorter period, exceeds EUR 30,000 must apply for registration by the 20th of the following month (arts. 42 and 55). A VAT-registered taxpayer cannot pay lump-sum PIT (PIT Law art. 49(12)). See the [VAT skill](montenegro-vat.md). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 160 | ## Deadlines and filing | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 162 | - **Annual return.** Form GPP-FL with schedules A, PPG, A1, B, B1, C, D, D1, E, E1, F, G and H ([return rulebook art. 3][return]), filed by the end of April for the previous year (PIT Law art. 43(2)). Tax is self-assessed and paid when the return is filed; advances and withholding on all sources are credited (art. 42(2), (4) and (5)). An overpayment is refunded on request or credited to the next year's advances (art. 42(6)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 163 | - **Who files** (art. 43(4) to (6)): residents with a principal independent activity (unless on lump-sum tax), supplementary entrepreneurship, or occasional work not taxed by withholding; rent, unless the payer is a legal person or entrepreneur; capital gains; foreign income; salary from two or more payers with total monthly gross above EUR 700; sport; copyright and related income; internet and gaming income. A licensed holiday-letting provider files even with no income. A nonresident files for Montenegrin income on which no withholding is prescribed. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 164 | - **Where and how.** A resident files with the tax office of his or her domicile, a nonresident with that of his or her stay, in person, by post or electronically; the taxpayer or an authorised person signs ([return rulebook arts. 4 and 5][return]; PIT Law art. 43(7)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 165 | - **Cessation.** A taxpayer who stops an independent activity files within 30 days of deletion from the register (art. 43(2a)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 166 | - **Late or missing return.** The tax authority files a return on the taxpayer's behalf ex officio (art. 43(6b)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 167 | - **Two or more employers.** The authority assesses the tax by decision within 30 days of the return, from the return and its own records (art. 43a). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 168 | - **Business advances.** Principal and supplementary business tax is paid in monthly advances of 1/12 of the previous year's tax, by the end of each month for the previous month (art. 48(1) and (2)). A new business files an information return with its estimated income within 15 days of registration (art. 48(4)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 169 | - **Lump-sum tax** (art. 49): available on request to a principal independent activity with turnover below EUR 30,000.01. Apply by the end of January, or within five days of registration for a new activity; the authority decides within 15 days. Tax is paid monthly at 1/12 by the end of the month for the previous month; seasonal activities of up to six months pay by the 5th of the month for the current month. Turnover books must be kept. Not available for lawyers, notaries, auditors, accountants, health, consulting, design, surveying, enforcement officers and other professional and intellectual activities, hairdressing, billiards and amusement games, wholesale and retail trade, hospitality, hotels, financial intermediation, real estate, or VAT-registered taxpayers; the authority can approve lump-sum tax for trade or hospitality in a kiosk, trailer or mobile unit only where the taxpayer produces and sells only its own products (art. 49(10) and (11)). If circumstances justify it, the authority orders a change of method by decision (art. 49(7)). The scale is set by Ministry rulebook (art. 49(2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 170 | - **Withholding by the payer, at payment:** salary (art. 46), occasional work (art. 48a), rent paid by a legal person or entrepreneur (art. 49a), capital income (art. 50), sport and copyright income earned in Montenegro (arts. 50b(1) and 50c(1)), other receipts (art. 50d). Gambling: the organiser withholds at payment and pays by the 15th of the following month (art. 50f). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 171 | - **Paid with the annual return:** capital gains (art. 50a), internet and gaming income (art. 50e), sport and copyright income earned abroad (arts. 50b(2) and 50c(2)), and occasional work or rent received directly (arts. 48a(2) and 49a(2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 172 | - **Self-assessed within five days:** salary from abroad, and salary from a foreign diplomatic mission or international organisation (art. 46(6) and (7)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 173 | - **Employer reports.** Monthly report of taxes and contributions by the 15th of the month for the previous month (art. 47(3)); annual statement of salary and tax to each employee by 31 January (art. 47(1)); other payers give recipients a statement by 31 January (art. 47(5)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 174 | - **Records.** Keep identity and residence evidence, income records, accounts and loss registers, asset, valuation and indexation evidence, relief decisions, foreign tax and treaty evidence, payer statements, advance payments, the signed return and payment records. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 176 | ## Penalties and interest | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 178 | - **Individual:** EUR 500 to 2,000 for not filing the return by the end of April (or within 30 days of cessation), wrong computation or not paying with the return; for a resident with foreign or diplomatic-mission salary not paying within five days; and for rent withholding failures (art. 53v). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 179 | - **Entrepreneur:** EUR 1,000 to 6,000 for not filing or paying the annual return, not paying advances on time, not filing the information return within 15 days of registration, not paying lump-sum tax on time, or not keeping turnover books; a ban on the activity of one to three months can be added (art. 53a(3) and (4)). For an individual, not keeping turnover books brings a EUR 200 misdemeanour order (art. 53b). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 180 | - **Legal-person payer:** EUR 2,000 to 20,000 for failing to compute, withhold or pay tax on salary, rent, capital, sport, copyright, other receipts or gambling winnings, or to give salary data or the monthly report; the responsible person EUR 500 to 1,000 (art. 53a(1) and (2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 181 | - **Interest, appeals, enforcement.** The PIT Law refers these to the tax procedure law (art. 53). Late-payment interest runs from the day after the due date: 0.03% a day until 17 July 2026, then 5.4% a year for 18 July to 31 December 2026 (ECB main refinancing rate plus 3 points, set each half-year) (Tax Administration Law art. 95, as amended by 104/26 arts. 20 and 26; Tax Administration decision of 24 July 2026). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 182 | - A fine follows a specific offence; an arithmetic difference is not by itself a penalty. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 184 | ## Cross-border points | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 186 | - **Nonresidents** are taxed only on Montenegrin income (art. 4(2)), with no loss carry-forward (art. 8). They file only for Montenegrin income on which no withholding is prescribed (art. 43(6)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 187 | - **Foreign tax credit.** A resident who pays income tax abroad gets a credit for it, capped at the Montenegrin tax on that income (art. 44). Compute the cap with the right class rate and base: a principal business uses the progressive bands, not a flat 15%. Do not credit social contributions, refundable treaty excess or unproved tax. The law gives no carry-forward of unused credit. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 188 | - **Treaties** prevail over the PIT Law (art. 45(1)). The Ministry sets the form and procedure for residence certificates (art. 45(2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 189 | - **Foreign salary** is self-assessed and paid within five days of receipt (art. 46(7)); a nonresident organisation with a seat or permanent unit in Montenegro withholds like a local employer (art. 46(5)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 190 | - **Foreign sport and copyright income** is taxed annually with the return (arts. 50b(2) and 50c(2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 191 | - **Posted workers.** An individual sent abroad by a Montenegrin resident or for an international organisation remains resident (art. 3(2)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 192 | - **Digital nomads.** Income from a foreign employer earned under digital-nomad status is exempt under art. 32d. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 193 | - **Payments to nonresidents in a business.** Interest above the usual commercial rate and payments to a nonresident head office are not deductible (art. 21(4) and (5)). | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 195 | ## Worked examples | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 197 | All figures are EUR for 2026. Facts are as stated; there is no surtax, social contribution, VAT or relief unless stated. | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 199 | ### 1. Principal business: accounts to tax, and the annual balance | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 201 | Total revenue 80,000; accounting profit 25,000 after all booked costs. Add back private costs 1,200 and book depreciation above tax depreciation 800. Qualifying-purpose spending 3,000 exceeds the 3% cap (2,400) by 600; representation 1,000 exceeds 1% (800) by 200; memberships 120 exceed 0.1% (80) by 40. Adjusted profit is 27,840. An unused business loss of 3,000 within its five years brings the base to 24,840. PIT is 3,600 × 9% = 324 plus 12,840 × 15% = 1,926, total 2,250. Advances paid 1,800, so 450 is due with the return. [Arts. 17 to 33, 42 and 48][law] | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 203 | ```oa-check | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 204 | name: Montenegro principal business annual profit and settlement | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 205 | inputs: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 206 |   revenue: 80000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 207 |   accounting_profit: 25000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 208 |   private_expense: 1200 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 209 |   depreciation_adjustment: 800 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 210 |   qualifying_spend: 3000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 211 |   representation: 1000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 212 |   memberships: 120 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 213 |   available_loss: 3000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 214 |   paid_advances: 1800 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 215 | steps: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 216 |   - purpose_excess: max(qualifying_spend - revenue * 0.03, 0) | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 217 |   - representation_excess: max(representation - revenue * 0.01, 0) | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 218 |   - membership_excess: max(memberships - revenue * 0.001, 0) | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 219 |   - adjusted_profit: accounting_profit + private_expense + depreciation_adjustment + purpose_excess + representation_excess + membership_excess | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 220 |   - loss_used: min(available_loss, max(adjusted_profit, 0)) | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 221 |   - taxable_business: max(adjusted_profit - loss_used, 0) | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 222 |   - annual_pit: max(min(taxable_business, 12000) - 8400, 0) * 0.09 + max(taxable_business - 12000, 0) * 0.15 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 223 |   - settlement_due: annual_pit - paid_advances | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 224 | expected: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 225 |   purpose_excess: 600 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 226 |   representation_excess: 200 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 227 |   membership_excess: 40 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 228 |   adjusted_profit: 27840 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 229 |   loss_used: 3000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 230 |   taxable_business: 24840 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 231 |   annual_pit: 2250 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 232 |   settlement_due: 450 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 233 | ``` | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 235 | ### 2. Principal versus supplementary business | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 237 | The same taxable profit of 10,000. As a principal activity, PIT is 1,600 × 9% = 144. As supplementary entrepreneurship, PIT is 15% = 1,500. The class follows the facts and registration; it is not an election. [Arts. 10, 12 and 16][law] | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 239 | ```oa-check | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 240 | name: Montenegro principal versus supplementary income rates | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 241 | inputs: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 242 |   taxable_profit: 10000 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 243 | steps: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 244 |   - principal_pit: max(min(taxable_profit, 12000) - 8400, 0) * 0.09 + max(taxable_profit - 12000, 0) * 0.15 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 245 |   - supplementary_pit: taxable_profit * 0.15 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 246 | expected: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 247 |   principal_pit: 144 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 248 |   supplementary_pit: 1500 | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 249 | ``` | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 251 | ### 3. Occasional work paid by a company | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 253 | Gross fee 2,000, no documented costs, paid by a company. Standard expenses 600, base 1,400, PIT withheld 210. The 1,790 left is before any contribution due on the fee. [Arts. 16(5), 20(2) and 48a][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 271 | ### 4. Rent with standard expenses | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 273 | Gross rent 20,000, no documented costs. Ordinary letting: base 14,000, PIT 2,100. Tourist letting with tourist tax paid: base 10,000, PIT 1,500. Tourist letting under an agency or local tourist organisation lease with at least 60 days' average occupancy: base 6,000, PIT 900. These are three different sets of facts. [Arts. 35, 36 and 49a][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 295 | ### 5. Property gain with a same-year capital loss | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 297 | Sale of a property that was not the only and main residence for 150,000, with documented reconstruction 7,000 and sale costs 3,000; the price is not below market. Indexed acquisition cost is 110,000. Adjusted sale price 140,000, gain 30,000. A capital loss of 5,000 on shares sold in the same year reduces the taxable gain to 25,000; PIT 3,750, paid with the return. [Arts. 37a to 37d, 37g, 37h and 50a][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 299 | ```oa-check | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 300 | name: Montenegro property gain and current capital loss | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 301 | inputs: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 302 |   sale_price: 150000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 303 |   reconstruction: 7000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 304 |   sale_costs: 3000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 305 |   established_indexed_cost: 110000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 306 |   current_capital_loss: 5000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 307 | steps: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 308 |   - adjusted_sale: sale_price - reconstruction - sale_costs | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 309 |   - gain: adjusted_sale - established_indexed_cost | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 310 |   - taxable_capital_gain: max(gain - current_capital_loss, 0) | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 311 |   - pit: taxable_capital_gain * 0.15 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 312 | expected: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 313 |   adjusted_sale: 140000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 314 |   gain: 30000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 315 |   taxable_capital_gain: 25000 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 316 |   pit: 3750 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 317 | ``` | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 319 | ### 6. Shares under FIFO | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 321 | Buy 100 shares at 10, then 100 at 15, of the same issuer; sell 150 at 20. FIFO cost is 100 × 10 + 50 × 15 = 1,750; gain 1,250; PIT 187.50. Fifty shares at 15 remain. [Arts. 37a, 37f and 50a][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 323 | ```oa-check | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 324 | name: Montenegro securities FIFO gain | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 325 | inputs: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 326 |   first_units: 100 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 327 |   first_price: 10 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 328 |   second_units: 100 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 329 |   second_price: 15 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 330 |   units_sold: 150 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 332 | steps: | Guide: unchanged exact line |

| 333 |   - first_used: min(first_units, units_sold) | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 334 |   - second_used: units_sold - first_used | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 335 |   - proceeds: units_sold * sale_price_each | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 336 |   - fifo_cost: first_used * first_price + second_used * second_price | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 337 |   - gain: proceeds - fifo_cost | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 338 |   - pit: gain * 0.15 | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 350 | ### 7. Internet income versus video-game income | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 352 | Two receipts of 10,000 each, neither salary nor taxed under another heading. Internet income: 15% expenses, base 8,500, PIT 1,275. Video-game competition income with no documented costs: 20% standard, base 8,000, PIT 1,200. [Arts. 37o to 37r and 50e][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 371 | ### 8. Foreign dividend with a capped credit | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |

| 373 | A resident receives a foreign dividend of 10,000 gross and proves 2,000 of foreign income tax paid. Montenegrin tax is 1,500, so the credit is capped at 1,500 and nothing is left to pay. The 500 excess is not carried forward, refunded or set against other income. [Arts. 37, 44 and 45][law] | Retained examples appendix: unchanged exact line |
